Yeah… yeah, I guess I have. I used to look forward to things more. Choir practice, for one. And bridge—though some people take bridge too serious, like my aunt used to, she’d slap the table if you played the wrong card.

Yeah… people have said that. Not always in a mean way, but they’ll look at me like I left something out.

I don’t know… not really like that, I don’t think.

feeling, like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to choir practice, bridge, cooking beans on Sunday, and doing little repair jobs for folks, but now I mostly let the phone ring and tell myself there is no point going.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: I forget to eat I still felt like the neighbors were keeping track of my door, like when I came and went, and reporting it somehow. It wasn’t because I was down that week or anything. It was still going on, even when I felt a bit more… normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like that, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve had nights where I don’t sleep much, but that’s not the same as feeling full of energy. Usually if I’m up, I’m just up, and the next day I feel worn down. Sometimes I’ll be moving around the apartment, checking on things, and then I realize it’s later than I thought—like when the hallway light buzzes, it does that after midnight sometimes, or maybe I just notice it more then.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling great from it… no, I can’t say I remember that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, “seen” like with my eyes? Sometimes I think something moves in the corner, but then it’s just a coat or the curtain, you know.\n\nMy apartment has this old lamp, and when the bulb flickers it makes shadows jump on the wall, especially by the kitchen doorway. I keep meaning to get a new bulb, but the store moved things around and I don’t like hunting through those aisles.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say I see things other people can’t see. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Other strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly that same thing with the apartment.

Like, I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and somebody’s slipping notes about me to the office. People keep saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like rent stuff or pest control or whatever, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.

The office has those little stacks of papers sometimes, and once I saw one folded wrong—well, not wrong, but the way it was sitting made me think it had been handled. Anyway, that’s the main thing people keep telling me isn’t true.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F25.9, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because, uh… the people at my apartment, they’re watching when I come and go. I know they are. And they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on me or something. Everybody keeps saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” but that’s not what it feels like. They don’t see how it lines up.\n\nAnd the office, you know, they act polite, but I’ve lived in apartments a long time—different buildings, different managers—and you learn when people are keeping track. So… yeah, that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, when you say voices, like somebody talking right in the room? I can’t say that. Sometimes apartments make sounds, you know, pipes or people out in the hall, and I’ll stop and listen, but then it’s like—well, this building has thin walls. My old place was like that too, you could hear a TV through the vents and think it was your phone ringing.\n\nSo… I’m not sure that’s what you mean. I haven’t really noticed, like, a voice talking to me when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently had a period where you felt unusually high, energized, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Unusually high? I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I get up and try to get things done, like laundry or going through mail, but I wouldn’t say “on top of the world.” That sounds like… I don’t know, like winning the lottery or something.\n\nI drink coffee sometimes and then I’ll feel a little wired, but that’s different. So, no, I haven’t really noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you started using any drugs or medicines recently?\n\nPatient: New drugs or medicines… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve got the usual stuff around the apartment, like old bottles in the cabinet, and I try not to mess with things because then you forget what’s what. My mother used to keep everything in a shoebox, and half the labels would be worn off, you know?\n\nBut recently, like starting something new… I can’t really say I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if someone could put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my mind… I don’t know if I’d say it like that. It’s more like—people already seem to know things, or they’re keeping track.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. The people at my apartment, they watch when I leave and when I come back, and then there’s these notes, like slipped around or put up, and they say it’s just notices on the doors, but it feels like it’s about me. The office gets told things, I’m pretty sure. I mean, apartments always have rules about trash pickup and parking, and one place I lived they’d tape papers everywhere, but this is different.\n\nSo… not putting thoughts into my head exactly. More like they’re watching and writing about me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and reporting on you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I want to say a little over a year. Like about a year and a month, maybe.\n\nIt didn’t all start like one big thing, you know? At first I thought maybe it was just regular apartment stuff, people looking out their windows, the office putting papers on doors. Apartments are always like that—there’s always some notice about water shutoffs or trash or whatever. But this feeling that it was about me, that they were watching and reporting… that’s been going on about a year and a month.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself showing less emotion or feeling less interested in being around other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I guess I have. I used to look forward to things more. Choir practice, for one. And bridge—though some people take bridge too serious, like my aunt used to, she’d slap the table if you played the wrong card.\n\nBut I mean, I used to go. I used to cook beans on Sunday, make a whole pot, and sometimes I’d do little repair jobs for folks, fix a hinge or a shelf, that kind of thing. Lately I just… mostly let the phone ring. I tell myself there’s no point going, no point starting anything. So yeah, I’m not around people like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another in a way that doesn’t make sense to them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have said that. Not always in a mean way, but they’ll look at me like I left something out.\n\nLike I’ll try to explain what’s going on, and I start with the rent, because that’s where it makes sense to start, but then it connects to the marina—there was a thing down there with the boats—and then I’m talking about the choir robes, because those got moved, or maybe not moved, and then the wires in the walls, and people act like I jumped over half the story.\n\nI don’t mean to. In my head it’s connected. But when I say it out loud, I guess it comes out… scattered.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of feeling very depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n\nIt’s like before I even look out the window or make coffee—well, I don’t always make coffee right away because the pot’s been acting funny, the switch sticks sometimes—but anyway, I wake up and it’s already sitting there on me. That low feeling. And it keeps on most of the day, a lot of days.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when these experiences of people watching or reporting on you were happening even though you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that. I mean, I wasn’t waking up with that hopeless feeling so bad, and I was eating a little better for a few weeks there.\n\nBut the watching thing didn’t stop. I still felt like the neighbors were keeping track of my door, like when I came and went, and reporting it somehow. It wasn’t because I was down that week or anything. It was still going on, even when I felt a bit more… normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like that, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve had nights where I don’t sleep much, but that’s not the same as feeling full of energy. Usually if I’m up, I’m just up, and the next day I feel worn down. Sometimes I’ll be moving around the apartment, checking on things, and then I realize it’s later than I thought—like when the hallway light buzzes, it does that after midnight sometimes, or maybe I just notice it more then.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling great from it… no, I can’t say I remember that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, “seen” like with my eyes? Sometimes I think something moves in the corner, but then it’s just a coat or the curtain, you know.\n\nMy apartment has this old lamp, and when the bulb flickers it makes shadows jump on the wall, especially by the kitchen doorway. I keep meaning to get a new bulb, but the store moved things around and I don’t like hunting through those aisles.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say I see things other people can’t see. Nothing clear like that, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Besides people at your apartment watching and reporting on you, have you had any other strong beliefs that others said were not true?\n\nPatient: Other strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly that same thing with the apartment.\n\nLike, I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and somebody’s slipping notes about me to the office. People keep saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like rent stuff or pest control or whatever, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nThe office has those little stacks of papers sometimes, and once I saw one folded wrong—well, not wrong, but the way it was sitting made me think it had been handled. Anyway, that’s the main thing people keep telling me isn’t true.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… I don’t think I felt euphoric. That’s kind of a strong word.

When I think people are watching or reporting, I’m usually tense, not energetic in a good way. I might be up and walking around, checking the door or looking out, but that’s not like feeling great. It’s more like I can’t settle.

Sometimes I’ll start cleaning a little, like moving papers from one spot to another, and then I get distracted because I notice the dust by the vent, and that vent has been loose for months… I told maintenance once, but they don’t come when they say they will.

So, no, I don’t really remember a time where I felt unusually energetic or happy at the same time as all that.

My old place was like that too, you could hear a TV through the vents and think it was your phone ringing.\n\nSo… I’m not sure that’s what you mean. I haven’t really noticed, like, a voice talking to me when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently had a period where you felt unusually high, energized, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Unusually high? I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I get up and try to get things done, like laundry or going through mail, but I wouldn’t say “on top of the world.” That sounds like… I don’t know, like winning the lottery or something.\n\nI drink coffee sometimes and then I’ll feel a little wired, but that’s different. So, no, I haven’t really noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you started using any drugs or medicines recently?\n\nPatient: New drugs or medicines… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve got the usual stuff around the apartment, like old bottles in the cabinet, and I try not to mess with things because then you forget what’s what. My mother used to keep everything in a shoebox, and half the labels would be worn off, you know?\n\nBut recently, like starting something new… I can’t really say I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if someone could put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my mind… I don’t know if I’d say it like that. It’s more like—people already seem to know things, or they’re keeping track.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. The people at my apartment, they watch when I leave and when I come back, and then there’s these notes, like slipped around or put up, and they say it’s just notices on the doors, but it feels like it’s about me. The office gets told things, I’m pretty sure. I mean, apartments always have rules about trash pickup and parking, and one place I lived they’d tape papers everywhere, but this is different.\n\nSo… not putting thoughts into my head exactly. More like they’re watching and writing about me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and reporting on you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I want to say a little over a year. At first I thought maybe it was just regular apartment stuff, people looking out their windows, the office putting papers on doors. Apartments are always like that—there’s always some notice about water shutoffs or trash or whatever. But this feeling that it was about me, that they were watching and reporting… that’s been going on about a year and a month.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself showing less emotion or feeling less interested in being around other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I guess I have. I used to look forward to things more. Choir practice, for one. And bridge—though some people take bridge too serious, like my aunt used to, she’d slap the table if you played the wrong card.\n\nBut I mean, I used to go. I used to cook beans on Sunday, make a whole pot, and sometimes I’d do little repair jobs for folks, fix a hinge or a shelf, that kind of thing. Lately I just… mostly let the phone ring. I tell myself there’s no point going, no point starting anything. So yeah, I’m not around people like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another in a way that doesn’t make sense to them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have said that. Not always in a mean way, but they’ll look at me like I left something out.\n\nLike I’ll try to explain what’s going on, and I start with the rent, because that’s where it makes sense to start, but then it connects to the marina—there was a thing down there with the boats—and then I’m talking about the choir robes, because those got moved, or maybe not moved, and then the wires in the walls, and people act like I jumped over half the story.\n\nI don’t mean to. In my head it’s connected. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n\nIt’s like before I even look out the window or make coffee—well, I don’t always make coffee right away because the pot’s been acting funny, the switch sticks sometimes—but anyway, I wake up and it’s already sitting there on me. That low feeling. And it keeps on most of the day, a lot of days.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when these experiences of people watching or reporting on you were happening even though you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that. I mean, I wasn’t waking up with that hopeless feeling so bad, and I was eating a little better for a few weeks there.\n\nBut the watching thing didn’t stop. I still felt like the neighbors were keeping track of my door, like when I came and went, and reporting it somehow. It wasn’t because I was down that week or anything. My mother used to keep everything in a shoebox, and half the labels would be worn off, you know?\n\nBut recently, like starting something new… I can’t really say I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if someone could put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my mind… I don’t know if I’d say it like that. It’s more like—people already seem to know things, or they’re keeping track.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. The people at my apartment, they watch when I leave and when I come back, and then there’s these notes, like slipped around or put up, and they say it’s just notices on the doors, but it feels like it’s about me. The office gets told things, I’m pretty sure. I mean, apartments always have rules about trash pickup and parking, and one place I lived they’d tape papers everywhere, but this is different.\n\nSo… not putting thoughts into my head exactly. More like they’re watching and writing about me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and reporting on you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I want to say a little over a year. Like about a year and a month, maybe.\n\nIt didn’t all start like one big thing, you know? At first I thought maybe it was just regular apartment stuff, people looking out their windows, the office putting papers on doors. Apartments are always like that—there’s always some notice about water shutoffs or trash or whatever. But this feeling that it was about me, that they were watching and reporting… that’s been going on about a year and a month.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself showing less emotion or feeling less interested in being around other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I guess I have. I used to look forward to things more. Choir practice, for one. And bridge—though some people take bridge too serious, like my aunt used to, she’d slap the table if you played the wrong card.\n\nBut I mean, I used to go. I used to cook beans on Sunday, make a whole pot, and sometimes I’d do little repair jobs for folks, fix a hinge or a shelf, that kind of thing. Lately I just… mostly let the phone ring. I tell myself there’s no point going, no point starting anything. So yeah, I’m not around people like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another in a way that doesn’t make sense to them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have said that. Not always in a mean way, but they’ll look at me like I left something out.\n\nLike I’ll try to explain what’s going on, and I start with the rent, because that’s where it makes sense to start, but then it connects to the marina—there was a thing down there with the boats—and then I’m talking about the choir robes, because those got moved, or maybe not moved, and then the wires in the walls, and people act like I jumped over half the story.\n\nI don’t mean to. In my head it’s connected. But when I say it out loud, I guess it comes out… scattered.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of feeling very depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n\nIt’s like before I even look out the window or make coffee—well, I don’t always make coffee right away because the pot’s been acting funny, the switch sticks sometimes—but anyway, I wake up and it’s already sitting there on me. That low feeling. And it keeps on most of the day, a lot of days.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when these experiences of people watching or reporting on you were happening even though you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that. I mean, I wasn’t waking up with that hopeless feeling so bad, and I was eating a little better for a few weeks there.\n\nBut the watching thing didn’t stop. I still felt like the neighbors were keeping track of my door, like when I came and went, and reporting it somehow. It wasn’t because I was down that week or anything. It was still going on, even when I felt a bit more… normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like that, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve had nights where I don’t sleep much, but that’s not the same as feeling full of energy. Usually if I’m up, I’m just up, and the next day I feel worn down. Sometimes I’ll be moving around the apartment, checking on things, and then I realize it’s later than I thought—like when the hallway light buzzes, it does that after midnight sometimes, or maybe I just notice it more then.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling great from it… no, I can’t say I remember that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, “seen” like with my eyes? Sometimes I think something moves in the corner, but then it’s just a coat or the curtain, you know.\n\nMy apartment has this old lamp, and when the bulb flickers it makes shadows jump on the wall, especially by the kitchen doorway. I keep meaning to get a new bulb, but the store moved things around and I don’t like hunting through those aisles.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say I see things other people can’t see. Nothing clear like that, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Besides people at your apartment watching and reporting on you, have you had any other strong beliefs that others said were not true?\n\nPatient: Other strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly that same thing with the apartment.\n\nLike, I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and somebody’s slipping notes about me to the office. People keep saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like rent stuff or pest control or whatever, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nThe office has those little stacks of papers sometimes, and once I saw one folded wrong—well, not wrong, but the way it was sitting made me think it had been handled. Anyway, that’s the main thing people keep telling me isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric while the feeling that people were watching or reporting on you was also happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I felt euphoric. That’s kind of a strong word.\n\nWhen I think people are watching or reporting, I’m usually tense, not energetic in a good way. I might be up and walking around, checking the door or looking out, but that’s not like feeling great. It’s more like I can’t settle.\n\nSometimes I’ll start cleaning a little, like moving papers from one spot to another, and then I get distracted because I notice the dust by the vent, and that vent has been loose for months… I told maintenance once, but they don’t come when they say they will.\n\nSo, no, I don’t really remember a time where I felt unusually energetic or happy at the same time as all that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I keep meaning to get a new bulb, but the store moved things around and I don’t like hunting through those aisles.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say I see things other people can’t see. Nothing clear like that, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Besides people at your apartment watching and reporting on you, have you had any other strong beliefs that others said were not true?\n\nPatient: Other strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly that same thing with the apartment.\n\nLike, I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and somebody’s slipping notes about me to the office. People keep saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like rent stuff or pest control or whatever, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nThe office has those little stacks of papers sometimes, and once I saw one folded wrong—well, not wrong, but the way it was sitting made me think it had been handled. Anyway, that’s the main thing people keep telling me isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric while the feeling that people were watching or reporting on you was also happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I felt euphoric. That’s kind of a strong word.\n\nWhen I think people are watching or reporting, I’m usually tense, not energetic in a good way. I might be up and walking around, checking the door or looking out, but that’s not like feeling great. It’s more like I can’t settle.\n\nSometimes I’ll start cleaning a little, like moving papers from one spot to another, and then I get distracted because I notice the dust by the vent, and that vent has been loose for months… I told maintenance once, but they don’t come when they say they will.\n\nSo, no, I don’t really remember a time where I felt unusually energetic or happy at the same time as all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard a voice speaking to you when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: A voice… like somebody talking right to me?\n\nI don’t think so. I mean, sometimes the TV is on in the other room and I’ll think I heard something, but then it’s just the commercials getting louder. They do that on purpose, I swear, and the remote sticks unless you press it just right.\n\nBut if you mean a clear voice when nobody’s there… I haven’t really noticed that. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because, uh… the people at my apartment, they’re watching when I come and go. I know they are. And they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on me or something. Everybody keeps saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” but that’s not what it feels like. They don’t see how it lines up.\n\nAnd the office, you know, they act polite, but I’ve lived in apartments a long time—different buildings, different managers—and you learn when people are keeping track. So… yeah, that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, when you say voices, like somebody talking right in the room? I can’t say that. Sometimes apartments make sounds, you know, pipes or people out in the hall, and I’ll stop and listen, but then it’s like—well, this building has thin walls. My old place was like that too, you could hear a TV through the vents and think it was your phone ringing.\n\nSo… I’m not sure that’s what you mean. I haven’t really noticed, like, a voice talking to me when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently had a period where you felt unusually high, energized, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Unusually high? I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I get up and try to get things done, like laundry or going through mail, but I wouldn’t say “on top of the world.” That sounds like… I don’t know, like winning the lottery or something.\n\nI drink coffee sometimes and then I’ll feel a little wired, but that’s different. So, no, I haven’t really noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you started using any drugs or medicines recently?\n\nPatient: New drugs or medicines… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve got the usual stuff around the apartment, like old bottles in the cabinet, and I try not to mess with things because then you forget what’s what. My mother used to keep everything in a shoebox, and half the labels would be worn off, you know?\n\nBut recently, like starting something new… I can’t really say I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if someone could put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my mind… I don’t know if I’d say it like that. It’s more like—people already seem to know things, or they’re keeping track.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. The people at my apartment, they watch when I leave and when I come back, and then there’s these notes, like slipped around or put up, and they say it’s just notices on the doors, but it feels like it’s about me. The office gets told things, I’m pretty sure. I mean, apartments always have rules about trash pickup and parking, and one place I lived they’d tape papers everywhere, but this is different.\n\nSo… not putting thoughts into my head exactly. More like they’re watching and writing about me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and reporting on you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I want to say a little over a year. Like about a year and a month, maybe.\n\nIt didn’t all start like one big thing, you know? At first I thought maybe it was just regular apartment stuff, people looking out their windows, the office putting papers on doors. Apartments are always like that—there’s always some notice about water shutoffs or trash or whatever. But this feeling that it was about me, that they were watching and reporting… that’s been going on about a year and a month.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself showing less emotion or feeling less interested in being around other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I guess I have. I used to look forward to things more. Choir practice, for one. And bridge—though some people take bridge too serious, like my aunt used to, she’d slap the table if you played the wrong card.\n\nBut I mean, I used to go. I used to cook beans on Sunday, make a whole pot, and sometimes I’d do little repair jobs for folks, fix a hinge or a shelf, that kind of thing. Lately I just… mostly let the phone ring. I tell myself there’s no point going, no point starting anything. So yeah, I’m not around people like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another in a way that doesn’t make sense to them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have said that. Not always in a mean way, but they’ll look at me like I left something out.\n\nLike I’ll try to explain what’s going on, and I start with the rent, because that’s where it makes sense to start, but then it connects to the marina—there was a thing down there with the boats—and then I’m talking about the choir robes, because those got moved, or maybe not moved, and then the wires in the walls, and people act like I jumped over half the story.\n\nI don’t mean to. In my head it’s connected. But when I say it out loud, I guess it comes out… scattered.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of feeling very depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n\nIt’s like before I even look out the window or make coffee—well, I don’t always make coffee right away because the pot’s been acting funny, the switch sticks sometimes—but anyway, I wake up and it’s already sitting there on me. That low feeling. And it keeps on most of the day, a lot of days.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when these experiences of people watching or reporting on you were happening even though you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that. I mean, I wasn’t waking up with that hopeless feeling so bad, and I was eating a little better for a few weeks there.\n\nBut the watching thing didn’t stop. I still felt like the neighbors were keeping track of my door, like when I came and went, and reporting it somehow. It wasn’t because I was down that week or anything. It was still going on, even when I felt a bit more… normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like that, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve had nights where I don’t sleep much, but that’s not the same as feeling full of energy. Usually if I’m up, I’m just up, and the next day I feel worn down. Sometimes I’ll be moving around the apartment, checking on things, and then I realize it’s later than I thought—like when the hallway light buzzes, it does that after midnight sometimes, or maybe I just notice it more then.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling great from it… no, I can’t say I remember that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, “seen” like with my eyes? Sometimes I think something moves in the corner, but then it’s just a coat or the curtain, you know.\n\nMy apartment has this old lamp, and when the bulb flickers it makes shadows jump on the wall, especially by the kitchen doorway. I keep meaning to get a new bulb, but the store moved things around and I don’t like hunting through those aisles.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say I see things other people can’t see. Nothing clear like that, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Besides people at your apartment watching and reporting on you, have you had any other strong beliefs that others said were not true?\n\nPatient: Other strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly that same thing with the apartment.\n\nLike, I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and somebody’s slipping notes about me to the office. People keep saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like rent stuff or pest control or whatever, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nThe office has those little stacks of papers sometimes, and once I saw one folded wrong—well, not wrong, but the way it was sitting made me think it had been handled. Anyway, that’s the main thing people keep telling me isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric while the feeling that people were watching or reporting on you was also happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I felt euphoric. That’s kind of a strong word.\n\nWhen I think people are watching or reporting, I’m usually tense, not energetic in a good way. I might be up and walking around, checking the door or looking out, but that’s not like feeling great. It’s more like I can’t settle.\n\nSometimes I’ll start cleaning a little, like moving papers from one spot to another, and then I get distracted because I notice the dust by the vent, and that vent has been loose for months… I told maintenance once, but they don’t come when they say they will.\n\nSo, no, I don’t really remember a time where I felt unusually energetic or happy at the same time as all that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard a voice speaking to you when no one else was there?\n\nPatient: A voice… like somebody talking right to me?\n\nI don’t think so. I mean, sometimes the TV is on in the other room and I’ll think I heard something, but then it’s just the commercials getting louder. They do that on purpose, I swear, and the remote sticks unless you press it just right.\n\nBut if you mean a clear voice when nobody’s there… I haven’t really noticed that. Not like that.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past year, have these beliefs about people at your apartment watching and reporting on you been present almost all the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most of the time, I’d say. It’s been going on about a year and a month now, the thing with the apartment people and the office.\n\nI didn’t write down the exact day or anything, but it was around then, because I remember the weather was changing and I had this jacket with the zipper that kept catching—anyway, since then it’s been there a lot. Not every single second, but it doesn’t really go away.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
There was that time at the senior-center workshop… I guess that counts. They had us fixing this little shelf, you know, the kind with the cheap screws that strip if you turn them wrong. I started lining the screws up by size instead of fixing it, and I kept tapping the table.

The lady running it asked me to sit down, but I just kind of stood there holding the screwdriver. Like I couldn’t… switch to what she was asking. I wasn’t trying to be difficult. I just got stuck on the screws and the tapping, and everybody was looking at me like I was acting strange.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F25.9, F22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because, uh… the people at my apartment, they’re watching when I come and go. I know they are. And they’re slipping notes about me to the office, like reporting on me or something. Everybody keeps saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” but that’s not what it feels like. They don’t see how it lines up.\n\nAnd the office, you know, they act polite, but I’ve lived in apartments a long time—different buildings, different managers—and you learn when people are keeping track. So… yeah, that’s why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I mean, when you say voices, like somebody talking right in the room? I can’t say that. Sometimes apartments make sounds, you know, pipes or people out in the hall, and I’ll stop and listen, but then it’s like—well, this building has thin walls. My old place was like that too, you could hear a TV through the vents and think it was your phone ringing.\n\nSo… I’m not sure that’s what you mean. I haven’t really noticed, like, a voice talking to me when nobody’s there.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently had a period where you felt unusually high, energized, or on top of the world?\n\nPatient: Unusually high? I don’t know… I mean, I’ve had days where I get up and try to get things done, like laundry or going through mail, but I wouldn’t say “on top of the world.” That sounds like… I don’t know, like winning the lottery or something.\n\nI drink coffee sometimes and then I’ll feel a little wired, but that’s different. So, no, I haven’t really noticed a period like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you started using any drugs or medicines recently?\n\nPatient: New drugs or medicines… no, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve got the usual stuff around the apartment, like old bottles in the cabinet, and I try not to mess with things because then you forget what’s what. My mother used to keep everything in a shoebox, and half the labels would be worn off, you know?\n\nBut recently, like starting something new… I can’t really say I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt as if someone could put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: Put thoughts in my mind… I don’t know if I’d say it like that. It’s more like—people already seem to know things, or they’re keeping track.\n\nThat’s why I came in, really. The people at my apartment, they watch when I leave and when I come back, and then there’s these notes, like slipped around or put up, and they say it’s just notices on the doors, but it feels like it’s about me. The office gets told things, I’m pretty sure. I mean, apartments always have rules about trash pickup and parking, and one place I lived they’d tape papers everywhere, but this is different.\n\nSo… not putting thoughts into my head exactly. More like they’re watching and writing about me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and reporting on you?\n\nPatient: It’s been… I want to say a little over a year. Like about a year and a month, maybe.\n\nIt didn’t all start like one big thing, you know? At first I thought maybe it was just regular apartment stuff, people looking out their windows, the office putting papers on doors. Apartments are always like that—there’s always some notice about water shutoffs or trash or whatever. But this feeling that it was about me, that they were watching and reporting… that’s been going on about a year and a month.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself showing less emotion or feeling less interested in being around other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, I guess I have. I used to look forward to things more. Choir practice, for one. And bridge—though some people take bridge too serious, like my aunt used to, she’d slap the table if you played the wrong card.\n\nBut I mean, I used to go. I used to cook beans on Sunday, make a whole pot, and sometimes I’d do little repair jobs for folks, fix a hinge or a shelf, that kind of thing. Lately I just… mostly let the phone ring. I tell myself there’s no point going, no point starting anything. So yeah, I’m not around people like I used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another in a way that doesn’t make sense to them?\n\nPatient: Yeah… people have said that. Not always in a mean way, but they’ll look at me like I left something out.\n\nLike I’ll try to explain what’s going on, and I start with the rent, because that’s where it makes sense to start, but then it connects to the marina—there was a thing down there with the boats—and then I’m talking about the choir robes, because those got moved, or maybe not moved, and then the wires in the walls, and people act like I jumped over half the story.\n\nI don’t mean to. In my head it’s connected. But when I say it out loud, I guess it comes out… scattered.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of feeling very depressed most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling, like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor.\n\nIt’s like before I even look out the window or make coffee—well, I don’t always make coffee right away because the pot’s been acting funny, the switch sticks sometimes—but anyway, I wake up and it’s already sitting there on me. That low feeling. And it keeps on most of the day, a lot of days.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a time when these experiences of people watching or reporting on you were happening even though you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that. I mean, I wasn’t waking up with that hopeless feeling so bad, and I was eating a little better for a few weeks there.\n\nBut the watching thing didn’t stop. I still felt like the neighbors were keeping track of my door, like when I came and went, and reporting it somehow. It wasn’t because I was down that week or anything. It was still going on, even when I felt a bit more… normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Has there ever been a time when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like that, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve had nights where I don’t sleep much, but that’s not the same as feeling full of energy. Usually if I’m up, I’m just up, and the next day I feel worn down. Sometimes I’ll be moving around the apartment, checking on things, and then I realize it’s later than I thought—like when the hallway light buzzes, it does that after midnight sometimes, or maybe I just notice it more then.\n\nBut needing less sleep and feeling great from it… no, I can’t say I remember that happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people could not see?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, “seen” like with my eyes? Sometimes I think something moves in the corner, but then it’s just a coat or the curtain, you know.\n\nMy apartment has this old lamp, and when the bulb flickers it makes shadows jump on the wall, especially by the kitchen doorway. I keep meaning to get a new bulb, but the store moved things around and I don’t like hunting through those aisles.\n\nSo… I don’t know if I’d say I see things other people can’t see. Nothing clear like that, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Besides people at your apartment watching and reporting on you, have you had any other strong beliefs that others said were not true?\n\nPatient: Other strong beliefs… I mean, it’s mostly that same thing with the apartment.\n\nLike, I came in because I know they’re watching when I leave and when I come back, and somebody’s slipping notes about me to the office. People keep saying, “No, those are just notices on the doors,” like rent stuff or pest control or whatever, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nThe office has those little stacks of papers sometimes, and once I saw one folded wrong—well, not wrong, but the way it was sitting made me think it had been handled. Anyway, that’s the main thing people keep telling me isn’t true.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric while the feeling that people were watching or reporting on you was also happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think I felt euphoric.
